## 2026-05-07 - Mem0 OpenAI Pilot Import

Summary:
- Completed Mem0 setup with OpenAI as the active provider for the self-hosted staging instance.
- Switched the Mem0 LLM model from `gpt-5.4-nano` to `gpt-4.1-nano-2025-04-14` because the current Mem0 Chat Completions integration sends `max_tokens`, which newer GPT-5.4 nano rejects.
- Imported the 300-card OB1 Human Memory V2 pilot into Mem0 with `infer=false`.

Files touched:
- `recipes/shadow-cleanup/review_mem0_pipeline.py`
- `recipes/shadow-cleanup/test_review_mem0_pipeline.py`

Verification:

- Import smoke: 5 attempted, 5 succeeded.
- Pilot remainder: 295 attempted, 295 succeeded, 0 failed.
- Search checks using `filters={user_id, agent_id}` returned 3 results for opera/psychology, AI/workflows, and relationships/family-origin queries.
- `python -m pytest recipes\shadow-cleanup\test_review_mem0_pipeline.py -q` passed: 6 passed.

Decisions:
- Use `gpt-4.1-nano-2025-04-14` for compatibility and low cost in this Mem0 build.
- Keep `text-embedding-3-small` for embeddings.
- Continue using curated `infer=false` imports rather than raw-chat extraction.
- Use `filters` for Mem0 search; top-level `user_id`/`agent_id` are rejected by this Mem0 search API.

Next step:
- Review pilot retrieval quality, rotate the exposed generated Mem0 API key, then decide whether to import the remaining 1,889 curated cards.

## 2026-05-08 - Mem0 Extractor Apply Planner

## 2026-05-08 - Mem0 Extractor Batch Orchestrator

- Added `tools/mem0-extractor/batch_orchestrator.py`, a sequential resume-safe runner for larger OB1-to-Mem0 cleanup/import passes.
- The orchestrator chunks an input file, writes per-batch input/report/repaired-report/audit/apply/review files, records a manifest, skips completed batches on rerun, and requires explicit `--execute` for live writes.

## 2026-05-09 - Mem0 V1.2 Dream Maintenance Local Build

- Implemented local dry-run-first dream maintenance for hosted Mem0 MCP.
- Added `tools/mem0-remote-mcp/dream_maintenance.py` with flag parsing, grouping, deterministic dry-run planning, compact LLM pack generation, LLM action validation, and exact-ID guarded execution helpers.
- Added `tools/mem0-remote-mcp/dream_maintenance_cli.py` with `--audit-log`, `--output`, `--limit-groups`, `--execute`, `--max-deletes`, rollback log, and maintenance log options. Default mode is dry-run and writes a reviewable plan JSON.
- Planner behavior: high-confidence exact-ID `delete_candidate` -> delete action; weak delete -> keep; `stale_candidate` -> downgrade to historical/unknown; exact multi-ID `merge_candidate` -> merge; conflict -> ask_user.
- Live execution requires exact IDs, writes rollback rows before update/delete, writes results to a separate maintenance ledger, and refuses delete execution without `--max-deletes`.
- Verification: `python -m pytest tools\mem0-remote-mcp -q` -> 71 passed. `python -m py_compile tools\mem0-remote-mcp\dream_maintenance.py tools\mem0-remote-mcp\dream_maintenance_cli.py tools\mem0-remote-mcp\server.py` passed.
- Sample dry-run on `.local\sample-dream-audit.jsonl` wrote `.local\sample-dream-plan.json` with 2 actions: delete `mem-delete` and downgrade `mem-stale` to historical/unknown; no live execution was run.
- Deployment status: local only. Next step is decide whether to deploy/sync V1.1+V1.2 to VPS or continue with V1.4 retrieval quality locally.

## 2026-05-10 - Mem0 V1.6 Retrieval-Epoch Lifecycle Decay Local Build

- Implemented local V1.6 lifecycle decay for the hosted Mem0 MCP in `tools/mem0-remote-mcp/lifecycle.py`.
- Decay is based on retrieval epochs, not wall-clock time: one retrieval telemetry search event equals one epoch. If the memory system is unused, memories do not decay just because calendar time passed.
- Added `RetrievalAccessIndex` over `mem0-mcp-retrieval.jsonl`, tracking current retrieval epoch, per-memory access count, last seen epoch, and epochs since last access.
- Added dry-run lifecycle planning for `decay_low_use`: low-signal active memories can downgrade to historical only after enough retrieval epochs without being returned; historical memories can downgrade to cold after a larger epoch threshold; frequently retrieved historical/cold memories can promote upward.
- Protected psychology, relationships, family-origin, identity, opera, systems, major project, dossier, high-signal, and formative memories from automatic cold downgrade.
- Added rollback-guarded execution for lifecycle updates with mandatory `max_updates`, rollback JSONL, and maintenance JSONL.
- Added `tools/mem0-remote-mcp/lifecycle_cli.py` for scheduler-friendly dry-run/execute operation over a memory JSON export and retrieval telemetry log. Default is dry-run; execution requires `--execute --max-updates`.
- Updated Docker packaging and README lifecycle docs.
- Verification: `python -m pytest tools\mem0-remote-mcp -q` -> 99 passed. Runtime `py_compile` passed for all hosted MCP modules. Targeted lifecycle tests first failed on missing module/CLI, then passed after implementation.
- Deployment status: local only. Next step is deploy V1.6 to VPS after explicit approval, then add a safe memory-export source so the scheduled dry-run can evaluate the live memory set automatically.

## 2026-05-10 - Mem0 V1.11/V1.12 Query Router And Hybrid Retrieval Local Build

- Implemented local retrieval improvements in `tools/mem0-remote-mcp` based on Pinecone-style takeaways without switching backends.
- Added `QueryRoute` and `route_query()` in `retrieval.py` to classify searches as `skip`, `sensitive_deep`, `project`, `entity_exact`, or `normal`.
- Wired `Mem0Client.search()` to use routed candidate depth: trivial searches can short-circuit; sensitive relationship/psychology/family searches fetch a deeper candidate pool; project/infrastructure searches get routed domain boosts; backend candidate requests remain capped at 100 to avoid increasing worst-case load.
- Strengthened hybrid ranking with adjacent phrase overlap, expanded known entity terms, multi-domain coverage boosts, high-signal memory type boosts, low-signal generic note penalties, and junk-memory penalties for transcript artifacts such as speaker diarization/canonical-name sludge.
- Updated retrieval tests for query routing, sensitive candidate depth, and entity/domain ranking; updated README retrieval-quality docs.

## 2026-05-11 - Mem0/Context Hook Context Hygiene Local Fix

- Investigated hook context growth after visible `UserPromptSubmit` and context-mode hook blocks appeared in Codex conversations.
- Root cause: `tools/mem0-remote-mcp/client_hooks/mem0_context_hook.py` emitted visible `additionalContext` for `session_start`, `user_prompt`, and `post_compact`; Codex context-mode also injected the large `<context_window_protection>` block through `context-mode hook codex sessionstart`.
- Changed the Mem0 hook implementation so all routine modes return `{"suppressOutput": true}` while preserving background heartbeat and project-observation sends.
- Updated local active hook scripts at `~/.codex/mem0_context_hook.py` and `~/.claude/mem0_context_hook.py`; timestamp backups were created first.
- Removed the stale Codex `[features].codex_hooks` config line and disabled only the Codex context-mode hooks that can inject context (`SessionStart` and `UserPromptSubmit`). Kept non-context context-mode tool hooks active.
- Verification: targeted hook tests first failed against the old behavior, then passed; full `python -m pytest tools\mem0-remote-mcp -q` -> 148 passed; direct local hook smokes returned suppressed/empty outputs for routine Mem0 and remaining context-mode hooks.
- Decision: routine memory/context hooks should record telemetry and preserve external context stores, but should not add recurring text to the model context. Safety guardrails may still inject short warnings only on risky events.
- Next step: sync the same hook hygiene to VPS/ttyd configs only with explicit approval, then add observation diagnostics as V1.18.

## 2026-05-12 - Kontext V2 vs Mem0 MCP Reliability Evaluation

- Added a safe MCP-vs-MCP reliability harness in `tools/kontext-v2/kontext_v2/mcp_reliability_eval.py` plus tests in `tools/kontext-v2/tests/test_mcp_reliability_eval.py`.
- Harness compares live Kontext V2 MCP (`/api/v2/mcp/{token}`) against live Mem0 MCP (`/mcp/{token}`) using the existing V1.13 retrieval eval cases. It outputs aggregate metrics, failed case names, latencies, and IDs only by default; detailed row summaries are opt-in.
- RED check first failed on missing `kontext_v2.mcp_reliability_eval`; implementation then passed focused tests and full local suite.
- Remote live eval used Codex and Claude profile tokens from `/opt/mem0-remote-mcp/.env` without printing token values. No Mem0 writes, Kontext writes, deploys, or client cutover were performed.
- Results for both Codex and Claude profiles: Kontext V2 MCP passed 12/12; Mem0 MCP passed 12/12. Domain hits and memory-type hits were 12/12 for both.
- Latency from the VPS vantage point: Kontext averaged about 21-25 ms per MCP call; Mem0 averaged about 829-857 ms. Mem0's initialize response shape did not set the harness `init_ok` flag, but tools/list and search succeeded with zero errors.
- Overlap was low: shared-any 3/12 cases and shared-first 1/12. This means both pass the broad eval gates, but they often retrieve different top memories.
- Current decision: Kontext V2 looks competitive or faster for the narrow retrieval eval, but it is not yet a Mem0 replacement because the mirror has only 77 memories, lacks automatic continuous sync, lacks write/ingestion/update/project-observation tools, and has not passed real-client long-running usage.
- Recommended next step: build continuous mirror sync + mutation/ingestion parity, then run a larger eval set with exact expected IDs and freshness tests before any source-of-truth cutover.

## 2026-05-17 - Date-aware memory ranking and downgrade metadata

- Summary: Added date/tier-aware retrieval behavior for Mem0 and Kontext so current active memories can outrank older conflicting historical entries, and superseded entries are strongly demoted instead of deleted. Dream and lifecycle downgrades now write compact metadata explaining when and why tier/status changed.
- Files touched: `tools/mem0-remote-mcp/retrieval.py`, `tools/mem0-remote-mcp/test_retrieval_quality_fixtures.py`, `tools/mem0-remote-mcp/lifecycle.py`, `tools/mem0-remote-mcp/test_lifecycle.py`, `tools/mem0-remote-mcp/dream_maintenance.py`, `tools/mem0-remote-mcp/test_dream_maintenance.py`, `tools/kontext-v2/kontext_v2/retrieval.py`, `tools/kontext-v2/kontext_v2/repository.py`, `tools/kontext-v2/tests/test_retrieval_ranking.py`.
- Behavior: Retrieval now parses `effective_at`, `observed_at`, `updated_at`, `last_modified_at`, `created_at`, and `imported_at`; gives current active memories a bounded recency boost; penalizes `superseded_by`/`superseded_at` and superseded/outdated statuses; and keeps explicit historical/cold retrieval possible through filters. Kontext `list_memory_rows()` now returns timestamp columns for ranking.
- Maintenance metadata: Lifecycle tier changes add `lifecycle_action`, `lifecycle_updated_at`, `previous_memory_tier`, `previous_current_status`, `retrieval_access_count`, and `retrieval_epochs_since_access`. Dream stale downgrades add `downgraded_at`, `downgraded_by=dream_maintenance`, previous tier/status, `stale_reason`, and optional `maintenance_group_key`.
- Verification: TDD red run failed on the expected new assertions. Focused green run passed (`29 passed`). Full Mem0 local suite passed (`159 passed`). `py_compile` passed for all changed Python modules. Kontext pure ranking test passed (`3 passed`). Full local Kontext suite was not completed because local Postgres `127.0.0.1:55434` is unavailable and the broad pytest run timed out; the leftover pytest process was stopped. Live Mem0 retrieval eval was not run because `MEM0_API_KEY` is not set in this shell.
- Decision: Newer does not blindly win. The ranking preference is bounded to current active, non-superseded memories and still respects signal, domain, type, tier, and explicit filters.
- Next step: Deploy these Mem0/Kontext changes to the VPS, then rerun live retrieval parity/eval from the server environment where the service credentials already exist.

## 2026-05-19 - Kontext direct write-path parity started

- Summary: Implemented the first cutover gate for Kontext-as-Mem0 compatibility: when an MCP profile is explicitly `write_enabled`, direct Mem0-compatible write tools now apply to Kontext instead of only returning dry-run hashes. Dry-run-only profiles still return dry-run responses and do not mutate memory rows.
- Behavior: `save`, `update`, and `submit_memory_override` now route through `apply_intake_proposals(..., apply=profile.can_write)` and return safe hashed/summarized payloads with `mode=apply` plus `writes_applied`. `delete` still does not hard-delete; in write-enabled mode it records a pending `delete_candidate` flag. `flag_memory` records a pending flag in write-enabled mode and a dry-run flag in dry-run mode. `extract_memories` and `ingest_exchange` remain dry-run because agent-first exact writes are the preferred hot path.
- Files touched: `tools/kontext-v2/kontext_v2/mcp_bridge.py`, `tools/kontext-v2/tests/test_mcp_bridge_write_tools.py`, and `project_log.md`.
- Verification: new non-DB bridge tests passed: `python -m pytest tools/kontext-v2/tests/test_mcp_bridge_write_tools.py::test_dry_run_enabled_bridge_still_does_not_apply_direct_save tools/kontext-v2/tests/test_mcp_bridge_write_tools.py::test_write_enabled_bridge_applies_direct_save_update_override_and_flags_without_raw_content tools/kontext-v2/tests/test_mcp_contract_parity.py -q` -> `10 passed`. Syntax and whitespace checks passed: `python -m py_compile tools/kontext-v2/kontext_v2/mcp_bridge.py tools/kontext-v2/tests/test_mcp_bridge_write_tools.py`; `git diff --check -- tools/kontext-v2/kontext_v2/mcp_bridge.py tools/kontext-v2/tests/test_mcp_bridge_write_tools.py` produced no output.
- Not verified yet: DB-backed Kontext write tests still need a reachable local or VPS Postgres; the previous local run against DB-backed bridge tests timed out waiting for local database access.
- Next step: add/verify DB-backed write-mode tests against the VPS or local Kontext DB, then add a Mem0-compatible `mem0` profile/cutover canary route before switching any live client.
- Follow-up: Added a separate `mem0` compatibility MCP profile token path (`KONTEXT_MCP_MEM0_TOKEN` / `KONTEXT_MCP_MEM0_COMPAT_TOKEN`) so Kontext can later be canaried behind a Mem0-shaped MCP endpoint without stealing existing Codex/Claude/ChatGPT profile tokens. Verification expanded to `11 passed` including the Mem0-compatible profile contract test.

## 2026-05-19 - Kontext full LongMemEval predict-only run

- Summary: Expanded the LongMemEval predict-only gate from a 6-question smoke to a type-balanced capped sample and then a full 500-question public predict-only retrieval run. No answerer or judge model calls were used.
- Capped type sweep: ran 10 questions per official LongMemEval type against the deployed Kontext harness in an isolated temporary Postgres schema on the VPS. Results at top-k 50 and 200 were `60/60`; top-k 10 was `56/60`, and top-k 20 was `58/60`. All misses were classified as `evidence_below_cutoff`; schema was dropped.
- Full public predict-only run: ran all 500 LongMemEval questions in an isolated temporary schema with top-k cutoffs 10, 20, 50, and 200. Results: top-10 `465/500` (`0.93`), top-20 `484/500` (`0.968`), top-50 `495/500` (`0.99`), top-200 `500/500` (`1.0`), average search latency `1682.1 ms`; schema was dropped. Report paths inside the container: `/tmp/kontext-longmemeval-full/longmemeval_full_predict_1779213477/longmemeval_s-full-predict-only-predict-only-sweep.json` and `.md`.
- Miss analysis: top-50 had 5 misses, all `evidence_below_cutoff`: single-session-assistant 1, single-session-user 2, temporal-reasoning 2. Top-200 had no misses.
- Matrix update: `docs/kontext-v2-benchmark-matrix.md` now records the full LongMemEval predict-only results. Kontext exceeds Mem0's published LongMemEval top-50/top-200 percentages on retrieval evidence-hit rate, but this is not the same metric as Mem0's judged answer accuracy, so it should be treated as retrieval-gate evidence rather than an apples-to-apples judged-score win.
- Decision: LongMemEval retrieval coverage is strong enough to proceed. The next useful step is either inspect the five top-50 misses for low-top-k ranking improvements or design the first safe BEAM slice. Judged LongMemEval still requires explicit approval because answerer/judge model calls cost money.

A 60-question type sweep with tuned code still passed `60/60` at top-k 50 and 200.

Health after deploy returned `ok=true`, `mode=mirror_read_only`, `writes.enabled=false`, profiles include `mem0`.

- Decision: LongMemEval predict-only retrieval gate is now maxed at top-50/top-200. Treat this as retrieval evidence, not judged answer-score equivalence. Next step is designing the first safe BEAM slice.

## 2026-06-01 - Kontext BEAM selector rerank improves hard slice but still blocks gate

- Summary: After the OpenAI key was saved locally and validated without printing it, ran the approved BEAM6 hard offset-06 ladder steps under the `$0.20` cap. The existing dateparse/state path scored `3/6`; skipping BEAM current-state machinery for `information_extraction` restored the information-extraction case but still scored `3/6`; a compact focused-state fallback worsened to `2/6`, so it was made opt-in and kept out of the active ladder path.

- Verification: Local checks passed: `test_judged_benchmark_run.py` + `test_beam_failure_audit.py` (`79 passed`), pure benchmark adapter candidate/rank tests (`3 passed`), BEAM predict tests (`7 passed`), `py_compile`, and `git diff --check` with only existing line-ending warnings.
- VPS results: Deployed touched runner/audit/adapter files with backups under `/opt/kontext/backups/20260601T125903Z-beam-selector-rerank`. Regenerated private bundle `/opt/kontext/private/judged-bundles/beam-turnlevel-fourcat-rerank-20260601T130256Z-private.json` from the source BEAM rows. Fresh BEAM6 rerank run scored `4/6` (`accuracy=0.6667`, raw-payload hits `0`, actual cost about `$0.012924`). Wide prompt diagnostic also scored `4/6` with raw-payload hits `0`.
- Decision: Stop BEAM12/BEAM30. The remaining hard failures are one `preference_following` case and one `instruction_following` case; wider prompts did not help. Mem0 must remain backup/rollback. Next move should be a retrieval/evidence strategy change or a replacement gate decision, not another prompt-size retry.

## 2026-05-20 - Kontext BEAM 1M zero source-id fix

- Summary: Fixed the BEAM 1M 3-row predict-only miss from the expanded BEAM slice. Sanitized diagnostics showed one `information_extraction` miss with `evidence_not_retrieved`; the expected evidence source id was `0`, but the BEAM loader treated `0` as falsy and replaced it with the fallback turn index, so the evidence id could never match.
- Files touched: `tools/kontext-v2/kontext_v2/benchmarks/fixtures.py`, `tools/kontext-v2/tests/test_benchmark_beam_predict.py`, `docs/kontext-v2-benchmark-matrix.md`, and `project_log.md`.
- Fix: changed BEAM turn source-id normalization to preserve zero-valued ids and fall back to `index` only when `id` is `None` or blank. Added a regression fixture asserting `id: 0` remains source id `"0"` and matches `source_chat_ids: [0]`.
- Local verification: the regression failed before the fix as expected (`fallback-turn-id` instead of `"0"`). After the fix, `python -m pytest tools\kontext-v2\tests\test_benchmark_beam_predict.py -q` passed `4 passed`; `python -m py_compile tools\kontext-v2\kontext_v2\benchmarks\fixtures.py tools\kontext-v2\kontext_v2\benchmarks\beam_predict.py tools\kontext-v2\tests\test_benchmark_beam_predict.py` passed; `git diff --check` passed for touched files.
- Deploy: backed up remote `fixtures.py` to `/opt/kontext/backups/20260520T000036Z-beam-zero-source-id/fixtures.py`, copied the fixed file to `/opt/kontext/src/kontext_v2/benchmarks/fixtures.py`, copied `retrieval_eval_cases.v1.14-expanded.json` to `/opt/kontext/src/` for live eval reproducibility, rebuilt `kontext:latest`, and recreated only `kontext` and `kontext-worker`. Kontext DB volume, `/opt/mem0-remote-mcp/.env`, and Mem0 data were preserved.
- Remote verification: health inside the app container returned `ok=true`, `mode=mirror_read_only`, `writes.enabled=false`, profiles `chatgpt/claude/codex/mem0/perplexity`. Remote isolated-schema BEAM 1M 3-row rerun passed top-k 10/20/50/200 at `60/60` total questions, including 53 evidence-backed questions, with zero misses and avg search latency `1969.0 ms`; schema `beam_verify_1779235761` was dropped.
- Live MCP verification after deploy: Codex profile base eval scored Kontext `12/12` avg `629.37 ms` and Mem0 `12/12` avg `984.02 ms`; expanded eval scored Kontext `36/36` avg `682.78 ms` and Mem0 `36/36` avg `940.77 ms`. Kontext remains faster and equal on pass rate; no production cutover or live writes were enabled.
- Decision: The BEAM 1M 3-row retrieval miss was a parser/evidence-id bug, not a ranking failure. BEAM predict-only reporting should next separate non-evidence abstention questions from evidence-hit retrieval gates before larger BEAM sweeps.
- Next step: add explicit evidence-backed/skipped counts to benchmark reports, then run larger capped BEAM 100K/1M slices in isolated schemas.

## 2026-05-20 - Kontext BEAM 10M offset 6-8 miss diagnostics

- Summary: Verified remote hygiene first: Kontext health was `ok=true`, `mode=mirror_read_only`, writes disabled, with no leftover `beam10m_%`, `beam_diag_%`, or `beam_verify_%` schemas/processes. Then diagnosed the BEAM 10M offset 6 length 3 cap-60 top-k 50 misses with sanitized rank/features only.
- Results: The offset 6-8 slice currently scores top-k 10 `38/54`, top-k 20 `42/54`, top-k 50 `48/54`, and top-k 200 `54/54`, with 6 skipped no-match-rule rows. The six top-k 50 misses are instruction/preference-following rows where evidence exists below cutoff, not evidence-not-retrieved failures.
- Diagnostic finding: The first evidence session rows land at ranks 53-88, while exact turn rows are thousands deep. The common root cause is that the benchmark adapter heavily favors full session observations; exact local turn evidence is too weak, and common instruction/preference phrasing lets neighboring session distractors outrank the evidence sessions.
- Attempted fix: A benchmark-only synthetic local-window candidate experiment was implemented and deployed with backup `/opt/kontext/backups/20260520T090420Z-window-candidates/adapter.py`, but the isolated offset 6 sweep exceeded a 30-minute timeout, so the deployed adapter was rolled back from that backup. Cleanup dropped the temporary `beam10m_window_%` schema, and the service returned healthy in mirror/read-only mode.
- Local verification before rollback: focused non-DB adapter ranking tests passed `5 passed`; BEAM/reporting/LongMemEval loader checks passed `7 passed`; `py_compile` and `git diff --check` passed for the scoped files. The full local adapter suite still times out because local DB-backed tests depend on unavailable local Postgres.
- Decision: Do not keep all-window candidate expansion; it is too expensive for BEAM 10M. The next fix should improve local evidence targeting without multiplying the candidate set for every query, likely by query-time selective local reranking or a cheaper turn-context feature.

## 2026-05-20 - Kontext opt-in semantic BEAM reranker

- Summary: Added an opt-in benchmark-only semantic tail reranker for BEAM diagnostics. It keeps the existing lexical/session adapter as the default path, locks the top 40 base results, and reranks candidates 41-200 with `sentence-transformers/multi-qa-MiniLM-L6-cos-v1` using only query text plus candidate memory/context text. After embedding-cache deploy, the same smoke still passed with avg search latency about 24.5s.

- Local verification: `py_compile` passed for `adapter.py`, `beam_predict.py`, `locomo_predict.py`, and `beam_isolated_smoke.py`. Focused non-DB tests passed `10 passed`, covering semantic rerank/cache behavior, BEAM fixture handling, isolated smoke helpers, and default/session-only `_add_conversations` behavior.
- Deploy: backed up remote files to `/opt/kontext/backups/20260520T195200Z-beam-session-only`, deployed `locomo_predict.py` and `beam_predict.py`, rebuilt `kontext:latest`, and recreated only `kontext` plus `kontext-worker`; Kontext DB volume and Mem0 env/data were preserved.
- Remote verification: container `py_compile` passed. Health stayed `ok=True`, `mode=mirror_read_only`, `writes_enabled=False`, `dry_run=True`. Offset 6 session-only semantic smoke imported only 100 candidates, reduced add time from 104.9s to 12.0s and candidate load from 24.5s to 4.0s, and scored top-k 50/200 `4/4` on instruction/preference rows.
- Broader remote smoke: BEAM 10M offsets 0-9 instruction/preference rows with `session_only=True` and semantic rerank completed in 370s across 40 retrieval-evaluable questions. Results: top-k 10 `18/40`, top-k 20 `22/40`, top-k 50 `32/40`, top-k 200 `40/40`; weighted MRR top-k 50 `0.2291`, top-k 200 `0.2318`; no skipped rows and no leftover temp schemas. Health remained mirror/read-only.
- Follow-up rank diagnostics: sanitized offset 7-8 traces showed hard misses still have weaker lexical/adjacent features than distractor sessions, with evidence session ranks around 43-90. Semantic weight/lock simulations found an aggressive no-lock semantic config could improve top-10/top-20/MRR but did not improve total top-k 50 enough to justify replacing the default. A reciprocal-rank fusion between base and default semantic rankings was added as a small robustness improvement; local focused tests still passed `11 passed`.
- Deploy: backed up remote `adapter.py` to `/opt/kontext/backups/20260520T203500Z-semantic-rrf-fusion`, deployed the scoped file, rebuilt `kontext:latest`, and recreated only `kontext` plus `kontext-worker`; DB volume and Mem0 env/data were preserved.
- Remote RRF verification: container `py_compile` passed; health stayed `ok=True`, `mode=mirror_read_only`, `writes_enabled=False`, `dry_run=True`. Offset 9 session-only semantic smoke still scored top-k 50/200 `4/4`, with max rank 46 and avg search latency 6192.3 ms. Offset 7-8 hard smoke still scored top-k 50 `1/4` and `2/4`, top-k 200 `4/4` and `4/4`; no temp schemas remained. A full offsets 0-9 session-only semantic+RRF rerun completed in 299.2s across 40 retrieval-evaluable instruction/preference questions: top-k 10 `18/40`, top-k 20 `22/40`, top-k 50 `33/40`, top-k 200 `40/40`; weighted MRR top-k 50 `0.2294`, top-k 200 `0.2317`. Compared with pre-RRF session-only semantic, top-k 50 improved `32/40` -> `33/40`, while top-k 200 stayed perfect.
- Cross-encoder probe: `cross-encoder/ms-marco-MiniLM-L-6-v2` on offsets 7-8 improved those hard rows to at most top-k 50 `5/8`, but a full offsets 0-9 probe exceeded the 20-minute safety timeout. The process exited, cleanup found no leftover schemas, and health stayed mirror/read-only. Do not standardize cross-encoder rerank yet.
- Decision: Compact session-only mode makes broad BEAM 10M semantic diagnostics practical and preserves top-k 200 retrieval on the targeted instruction/preference slice. Semantic RRF is a small top-k 50 improvement, but top-k 50 quality is still weak on offsets 7-8. Next work should focus on a stronger fair reranker for hard instruction/preference evidence ranking, not more candidate expansion.

## 2026-05-21 - BEAM cross-encoder line-window diagnostic

- Summary: Added opt-in query-ranked line-window scoring for the benchmark-only cross-encoder reranker. Unlike fixed character windows, it splits session observations by message lines, ranks local line neighborhoods by query-token overlap, and scores the best local windows. This is intended to diagnose BEAM session-collapse failures without using evidence IDs, answers, raw output, or production writes.
- Files touched: `tools/kontext-v2/kontext_v2/benchmarks/adapter.py`, `tools/kontext-v2/tests/test_benchmark_adapter.py`, `docs/kontext-v2-benchmark-matrix.md`, and `project_log.md`.

A stricter RED test then caught that line windows were still short-circuited by the one-prefix-window default and did not reach a late relevant line. After fixing line windows to be independent of `text_windows`, `py_compile` passed and focused non-DB pytest passed `8 passed`. `git diff --check` passed with only existing LF/CRLF warnings.
- Deploy/verification: Backed up remote adapter to `/opt/kontext/backups/20260521T202313Z-cross-line-windows/adapter.py`, then redeployed the fixed adapter after backup `/opt/kontext/backups/20260521T202846Z-cross-line-windows-fix/adapter.py`. Remote health returned `ok=true`, `mode=mirror_read_only`, writes disabled, dry-run tools enabled. Remote `py_compile` passed and config smoke confirmed `line_windows` defaults to `0` and honors explicit opt-in.
- Remote diagnostics: The initial line_windows3 run before the independence fix preserved default ranks exactly and was not meaningful. The fixed line_windows3 hard offsets 7-8 report `/opt/kontext/reports/beam-cross-line-windows3-fixed-hard78-diagnostic-20260521T203046Z.json` scored ranks `[66, 26, 49, 23, 81, 20, 7, 35]`, top-k50 `6/8`, top-k200 `8/8`, MRR50 `0.0405`. - Combined profile: text_windows4 plus line_windows3 wrote `/opt/kontext/reports/beam-cross-text4-line3-hard78-diagnostic-20260521T203851Z.json` and matched the fixed-window profile exactly: ranks `[32, 28, 49, 65, 84, 14, 7, 12]`, top-k50 `6/8`, top-k200 `8/8`, MRR50 `0.0481`.

## 2026-05-21 - Judged report verifier gate

## 2026-05-21 - Judged runner auto-verification

- Summary: Integrated the judged verifier into `tools/kontext-v2/scripts/judged_benchmark_run.py` via `--verification-output` and `--verify-*` flags, so a future approved paid run writes both the judged result and its acceptance-gate verification report in one command.

## 2026-05-21 - BEAM row-isolated turn-level diagnostic

- Summary: Added `--conversations` support to `tools/kontext-v2/scripts/beam_rank_diagnostics.py` so cached multi-row BEAM files can be sliced by row index during sanitized diagnostics.
- Reason: A first turn-level check reused `/app/benchmark-data/beam_10M_rows_7_2.json` with `--offset 8`, but the loader ignores offset when the dataset path already exists. This meant the command repeated the first filtered row instead of isolating row 8. The fix passes `args.conversations` through to `_fixture_for_run`, which already supports row-index filtering.
- TDD/local verification: Updated the diagnostic test first; it failed because `fixture_conversations` was still `None`. After implementation, focused diagnostic tests passed `4 passed`; combined diagnostic plus gate tests passed `8 passed`; `py_compile` and scoped `git diff --check` passed with only the existing `project_log.md` LF/CRLF warning.

- Decision: Turn-level indexing is not the missing fair signal for BEAM hard offset 7-8. It still combines to top-k50 `6/8`, and combined MRR50 is below the session-only cross baseline. Keep session-only cross as the current best no-API diagnostic mode.

## 2026-05-22 - Kontext retrieval latency optimization

- Summary: Root-caused the new real-world Kontext shadow latency spike. The slow path was not Postgres or network time; it was Python-side retrieval scoring over large candidate pools, made worse by giant mirrored memory rows. The clean fix keeps the quality scorer but feeds it a smaller DB-assisted candidate set and bounds oversized body text for scoring.
- Files touched: `tools/kontext-v2/kontext_v2/retrieval.py`, `tools/kontext-v2/kontext_v2/repository.py`, `tools/kontext-v2/tests/test_retrieval_ranking.py`, and `project_log.md`.
- Implementation: Added cross-request prepared-row caching, per-query scoring context reuse, DB-assisted candidate merge using Postgres FTS plus high-signal fallback, `search_rows` timestamp fields for cache/recency, and bounded scoring text (`12000` chars title/head/tail) so very large memories do not dominate cold latency. Final candidate settings are DB FTS `1500`, high-signal fallback `700`, minimum DB-assisted candidates `200`.
- Local verification: Focused latency/ranking tests passed, full `test_retrieval_ranking.py` passed `24 passed`, targeted benchmark adapter tests passed `3 passed`, `py_compile` passed, and scoped `git diff --check` passed with only the existing LF/CRLF warning.
- Remote deployment: Backed up changed remote files under `/opt/kontext/backups/latency-*`, copied only touched Python files into `/opt/kontext/src` and the running `kontext`/`kontext-worker` containers, verified container `py_compile`, committed `kontext:latest`, and force-recreated only `kontext` plus `kontext-worker`. Kontext DB volumes and `/opt/mem0-remote-mcp/.env` plus `/opt/mem0-remote-mcp/data` were preserved.
- Remote latency evidence: Before optimization, profiling showed `list_memory_rows(10000)` around `416ms` but total `search_memories` around `8.8s`; cProfile attributed most time to `score_row`/`prepare_score_row`/tokenization. After final deploy, direct HTTP for the same project query measured cold `1929ms`, warm `1083ms` then `1014ms`; in-process measured cold `1952ms`, warm `1065ms`.
- Quality evidence: Live expanded MCP eval `/opt/kontext/reports/expanded-mcp-eval-latency-balanced-20260522T191029Z.json` passed for Kontext `36/36`, first-satisfying MRR `1.0`, no errors. Mem0 in the same run passed `35/36`, first-satisfying MRR `0.8704`. Kontext avg latency was `1096.66ms`; Mem0 avg latency was `1014.73ms`.
- Real-world shadow evidence: A normal Mem0 MCP search after the final deploy wrote a clean sanitized shadow row with `mem0_count=5`, `kontext_count=5`, no Kontext error, `shared_any=true`, Mem0 latency `1298.03ms`, and Kontext latency `1033.045ms`. The cutover observer remains green with `pass=9`, `warn=0`, `fail=0`, recommendation `continue_kontext_primary_canary`; its all-history latency average still includes older pre-fix slow rows.
- Decision: The 10s Kontext latency regression is fixed. Kontext is now quality-better and latency-same-class with Mem0 on live expanded evals, with fresh real-world shadow evidence showing Kontext faster on the latest row. Keep Kontext shadow/canary-only until explicit production cutover approval.

## 2026-05-24 - Claude custom connector compatibility fix

- Summary: Fixed the VPS-hosted Kontext MCP endpoint for Claude.ai/Claude Desktop custom connector validation. Claude Code's CLI accepted the simple JSON-RPC HTTP shape, but the web connector path needs stricter remote MCP/Streamable HTTP compatibility.
- Root cause: Public `OPTIONS` preflight returned `405`, and `GET` with `Accept: text/event-stream` returned JSON `200`. The MCP Streamable HTTP spec requires either SSE or `405 Method Not Allowed` for that GET shape.
- Files touched: `tools/kontext-v2/kontext_v2/http_api.py`, `tools/kontext-v2/kontext_v2/mcp_bridge.py`, and `tools/kontext-v2/tests/test_mcp_bridge.py`.

- Deploy: Backed up remote `http_api.py` and `mcp_bridge.py`, copied patched files to `/opt/kontext/src`, rebuilt `kontext:latest` from `/opt/kontext/src/Dockerfile`, and recreated only `kontext` plus `kontext-worker`. Kontext DB volumes and Mem0 env/data were preserved.
- Public route check: Using the existing Claude-profile URL without printing the token, `OPTIONS` from `https://claude.ai` now returns `200` with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: https://claude.ai`; `GET` with `Accept: text/event-stream` returns `405` with `Allow: POST, OPTIONS`; JSON probe GET returns `200`; JSON-RPC `initialize` POST returns `200`.
- Decision: Retry adding the same copied Kontext URL in Claude's custom connector modal. If Claude still fails, the next suspect is external reachability from Anthropic IP ranges or an OAuth expectation, not the basic endpoint protocol.

## 2026-05-26 - Kontext embedding footgun + shadow report breakdowns + cutover decision refresh

- Summary: Implemented the next safe Mastermind batch covering three independent surfaces that were called out as unresolved in the live state audit: (1) the legacy `VECTOR(16) NOT NULL` embeddings placeholder, which had zero read/write code paths but would crash any future batch embedding job, (2) the shadow telemetry report, which aggregated coarse counts but not enough to drive scoring decisions, and (3) the cutover decision artifact, which had a stale single-entry decision log that did not reflect the four already-shipped batches.
- Files touched: `tools/kontext-v2/kontext_v2/schema.py`, `tools/kontext-v2/scripts/retrieval_shadow_report.py`, `tools/kontext-v2/tests/test_schema.py`, `tools/kontext-v2/tests/test_retrieval_shadow_report.py`, `tools/kontext-v2/cutover_decision.md`, and `project_log.md`.
- Behavior: Schema appends idempotent `ALTER TABLE embeddings` statements that drop NOT NULL on the placeholder `embedding` column, add safe defaults to the legacy text placeholder columns, and add `embedding_dirty BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT TRUE` plus `embedding_text_hash TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT ''` so a future batch embedding job can tie stored vectors to source text and find rows that still need a real vector. Real embeddings are still NOT enabled; the change only stops the placeholder from being a silent footgun. The shadow report builder now exposes `zero_result_features`, `high_latency_features` (with the p95 threshold surfaced), and `repeated_query_top_n` so the user can see which query feature classes (autobiographical, temporal, current_state, length_bucket) are failing or slow on real production traffic. Cutover decision file refreshed with current status legend, embedding architecture rule, and a five-entry decision log capturing the actual shipped batches.
- TDD/local verification: Red tests first failed on the missing schema ALTER statements and on the missing `zero_result_features` / `high_latency_features` / `repeated_query_top_n` keys. After implementation, focused `test_schema.py` plus `test_retrieval_shadow_report.py` ran `4 passed`. Broader regression across `test_schema`, `test_retrieval_shadow_report`, `test_retrieval_shadow`, `test_retrieval_ranking`, `test_retention_policy`, `test_protected_history_retention`, `test_regression_canary`, and `test_mcp_shadow_logging` ran `40 passed, 3 failed`; the 3 failures are pre-existing DB-gated tests (`localhost:55434` is unavailable because Docker Desktop is not running locally, matching the established pattern). `py_compile` passed for all touched Python files. `git diff --check` passed for all touched paths.
- Safety: Schema ALTERs are idempotent (`DROP NOT NULL` is no-op when already nullable, `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` is safe). The embeddings table has zero rows in production today and zero read/write code paths, so the migration touches dead data. Shadow report changes are read-only and the new breakdowns deliberately count flags only — no raw query, memory text, or result IDs are added to the output (verified by the existing privacy assertions in the report test plus the new test's `rendered` substring checks).
- Deploy: Not executed from this session. Recommended deploy: backup `/opt/kontext/backups/<timestamp>-embedding-footgun-and-shadow-breakdowns`, copy patched `kontext_v2/schema.py` and `scripts/retrieval_shadow_report.py` into `/opt/kontext/src` (and `/opt/kontext/scripts`), rebuild `kontext:latest` hotpatch image, recreate only `kontext` and `kontext-worker`, then run `apply_schema` inside the container to apply the new ALTER statements. Preserve `/opt/kontext/.env`, `docker-compose.yml`, Kontext DB volumes, `/opt/mem0-remote-mcp/.env`, and `/opt/mem0-remote-mcp/data`.
- Decision: This batch closes one of the six unresolved areas (`embedding VECTOR(16) footgun`) and meaningfully advances another (`telemetry-driven retrieval scoring improvements` — the breakdowns are the foundation that scoring calibration needs). The remaining unresolved areas are: real graph extraction beyond `_derived_relations`, larger judged benchmark proof (n > 3), automated cutover readiness decision (the observer already recommends; the flag flip is a human decision), and dashboard `body=text` clarity (Mastermind U1 still unshipped).

## 2026-05-30 - Kontext temporal timeline normalization runner patch

- Summary: Added a real temporal timeline normalization layer to the judged runner before any further Mem0 retirement decision. The patch remains benchmark-runner scoped: no retrieval scoring, schema, MCP, dashboard, DB, or Mem0 code was changed.
- Files touched: `tools/kontext-v2/scripts/judged_benchmark_run.py`, `tools/kontext-v2/tests/test_judged_benchmark_run.py`, and this `project_log.md`.
- Behavior: Temporal prompts now build a `Temporal timeline:` before `Retrieved memories:`. The timeline normalizes `session_date` values such as `8:00 AM on May 9, 2023` to `2023-05-09`, resolves only safe relative terms (`yesterday`, `today`, `tomorrow`) from that date, orders events by parsed date/session order, emits capped source-id previews, date-candidate counts, relative resolutions, and a compact snippet. Temporal prompts also default memory excerpts to `900` chars when no explicit cap is passed. Non-temporal prompts remain unchanged.
- Verification: TDD red phase first failed on missing `parse_session_date`, missing `temporal_timeline_lines`, and missing `Temporal timeline:` prompt placement. After implementation, `test_judged_benchmark_run.py` passed `20 passed`; the broader requested judged/public-report regression passed `33 passed`; touched Python files compiled; and scoped `git diff --check` passed with only the known CRLF warning on `project_log.md`.
- Deploy: Backed up the VPS host and container runner copies under `/opt/kontext/backups/20260529T214651Z-judged-runner-timelinev2`, copied the patched runner to `/opt/kontext/scripts/judged_benchmark_run.py`, `/opt/kontext/src/scripts/judged_benchmark_run.py`, and `/app/scripts/judged_benchmark_run.py` in the running `kontext` container, then compiled all copies. Remote `.env`, docker compose, DB volumes, `/opt/mem0-remote-mcp/.env`, and `/opt/mem0-remote-mcp/data` were not touched.
- No-call smoke: Container synthetic smoke passed aggregate booleans: timeline present, timeline before retrieved memories, relative date resolved, temporal memory compacted, helper API present, and prompt size bounded.
- Paid validation attempt: Created fresh private `0600` chunk bundles `locomo-temporal10-timelinev2-20260529T214931Z-chunk1/2` from the existing temporal10 evidence-map slice. The first GPT-5.4-nano chunk attempted through the configured OpenRouter runtime path but returned HTTP `402` before any token usage; the sanitized blocker summary is `/opt/kontext/reports/judged-plans/locomo-temporal10-timelinev2-20260529T214931Z-blocked-summary.json`. The local direct OpenAI env key also returned HTTP `401` on the model-list check. Chunk 2 was not run because no valid paid provider path was available.
- Cutover decision: No LoCoMo30 or official n30 rerun is justified from this batch because the temporal10 timeline validation did not execute. Mem0 remains backup/rollback. The next blocker is operational provider auth/billing for the temporal10 paid validation, not a new code blocker in the runner.
- Paid retry: After the user copied a valid OpenAI key, validated it without printing the key and reran the staged timelinev2 temporal10 chunks with CR-safe key handling. Combined sanitized summary: `/opt/kontext/reports/judged-plans/locomo-temporal10-timelinev2-20260529T223409Z-combined-paid-summary.json`. Aggregate result: cutoff 20 passed `1/10` (`0.10`, avg score `0.12`); cutoff 200 passed `4/10` (`0.40`, avg score `0.42`). Usage was `381,843` total tokens; estimated cost was about `$0.01436`. The result is worse than the previous evidence-map temporal10 k20 result and far below the `>=8/10` threshold, so prompt/formatting work should stop here. No LoCoMo30 or n30 rerun is justified. Mem0 remains backup/rollback.

## 2026-05-30 - Kontext temporal parser correction and strategy rollback

- Summary: Continued the cutover loop from live state and found a concrete temporal metadata parser bug: the staged LoCoMo temporal10 private bundle uses day-month timestamps such as the masked shape `#/#:## AM on # Month, ####`, while the runner only parsed month-day timestamps. The prior timelinev2 paid run therefore had `0` parsed session dates in the actual private slice even though the synthetic smoke passed.
- Files touched: `tools/kontext-v2/scripts/judged_benchmark_run.py`, `tools/kontext-v2/tests/test_judged_benchmark_run.py`, and this `project_log.md`.
- Behavior: `parse_session_date()` now handles both `8:00 AM on May 9, 2023` and `8:00 AM on 9 May, 2023`. Temporal timeline lines now resolve relative terms correctly from the actual LoCoMo metadata shape. Non-temporal behavior is unchanged.
- Verification: TDD red phase failed on the day-month parser and prompt relative-resolution tests before implementation. After implementation, `test_judged_benchmark_run.py` passed `22 passed`; broader judged/reporting regression passed `35 passed`; touched Python files compiled; and scoped `git diff --check` passed with only the known `project_log.md` CRLF warning.
- Deploy: Backed up the VPS runner under `/opt/kontext/backups/20260529T225945Z-judged-runner-day-month-parse`, deployed the parser fix to `/opt/kontext/scripts`, `/opt/kontext/src/scripts`, and the running `kontext` container, then compiled all copies. Container smoke proved day-month parsing, relative resolution, and timeline ordering.
- Paid parser-fix validation: Reran the staged temporal10 timelinev2 chunks with GPT-5.4-nano under the `$0.12` cap. Combined sanitized summary: `/opt/kontext/reports/judged-plans/locomo-temporal10-timelinev2-20260529T230021Z-combined-paid-summary.json`. Result: k20 `5/10` (`0.50`, avg score `0.49`) and k200 `5/10` (`0.50`). Usage was `384,992` tokens; estimated cost was about `$0.01436`. This is a real improvement over prior timelinev2 k20 `1/10`, but still below the `>=8/10` threshold and at the stop boundary for this strategy.
- Negative follow-up: Tested a temporal-only snippet-reduction strategy because diagnostics showed high date ambiguity and very large relative-term density. It worsened results. Sanitized summary: `/opt/kontext/reports/judged-plans/locomo-temporal10-timelinev2-20260529T230508Z-combined-paid-summary.json`; k20 `2/10`, k200 `3/10`; usage `330,948` tokens; estimated cost about `$0.01436`.
- Rollback: Removed the snippet-reduction behavior locally, reran regression (`35 passed`), and redeployed the parser-only runner. Backup of the regressed snippet runner: `/opt/kontext/backups/20260529T230757Z-judged-runner-restore-parser-only`. Container smoke confirms the deployed runner keeps day-month parsing and 900-char temporal memory windows.
- Cutover decision: No LoCoMo30 or official n30 rerun is justified yet. Best current temporal10 k20 is `5/10`, below the `>=8/10` approval threshold. Mem0 remains backup/rollback. Next useful strategy should not be more prompt formatting; it should test deeper temporal extraction/model capability, for example a structured pre-answer temporal fact extraction step or a stronger-model diagnostic on the same temporal10 slice under a separate approved cap.

## 2026-05-30 - Kontext temporal candidate-date diagnostic and cutoff ablation

- Summary: Continued the active cutover loop from live state and tested one more evidence-driven temporal strategy: deterministic candidate-date extraction from relative phrases. The official judged n>=30 blocker remains open; Mem0 remains backup/rollback.
- Files touched: `tools/kontext-v2/scripts/judged_benchmark_run.py`, `tools/kontext-v2/tests/test_judged_benchmark_run.py`, and this `project_log.md`.
- Behavior: Temporal prompts now include `Temporal candidate dates:` before `Temporal timeline:`. The runner resolves safe day phrases such as `day before`, `previous day`, `next day`, and `following day` from parsed session dates, aggregates candidate dates by best rank/evidence count/session date, and keeps the candidate-date snippet short so temporal prompts stay bounded. Non-temporal prompts remain unchanged.
- Verification: TDD red phase failed on the missing safe day-phrase resolver, missing `temporal_candidate_date_lines()`, and missing prompt section. After implementation, `test_judged_benchmark_run.py` passed `24 passed`; focused judged/reporting regression passed `37 passed`; touched Python files compiled; and scoped `git diff --check` passed with only the known `project_log.md` CRLF warning.
- Deploy: Backed up the VPS runner under `/opt/kontext/backups/20260529T231736Z-judged-runner-temporal-candidates`, deployed the candidate-date runner to `/opt/kontext/scripts`, `/opt/kontext/src/scripts`, and the running `kontext` container, then compiled host/container copies. Container smoke proved candidate dates, timeline order, `yesterday`/`next day` resolution, and bounded prompt size. Remote `.env`, docker compose, DB volumes, `/opt/mem0-remote-mcp/.env`, and `/opt/mem0-remote-mcp/data` were not touched.
- Paid candidate-date validation: Reran the staged temporal10 timelinev2 chunks with GPT-5.4-nano under the `$0.12` cap. Combined sanitized summary: `/opt/kontext/reports/judged-plans/locomo-temporal10-timelinev2-20260529T231810Z-combined-paid-summary.json`. Result: k20 `5/10` (`0.50`, avg score `0.54`) and k200 `6/10` (`0.60`). Usage was `398,452` tokens; estimated cost about `$0.01436`. This is not enough to justify LoCoMo30 or official n30.
- Sanitized diagnostics: Failure audits for the two chunks show raw-payload key hits `0`. Chunk 1 was weak (`1/5` at k20, `2/5` at k200); chunk 2 reached target locally (`4/5` at both cutoffs). Aggregate private diagnostics, emitted only as counts/hashes, show all k20 failures still had first-hit evidence in ranks `1-5`, parseable dates were available, but every failed question had `6-10` relative candidate dates. Ground-truth date coverage showed candidate dates did not include the correct parseable date for the failed parseable-date questions.
- Cutoff ablation: Ran a separate k5/k10 temporal10 diagnostic using the same runner and GPT-5.4-nano under the same cap. Combined sanitized summary: `/opt/kontext/reports/judged-plans/locomo-temporal10-cutoffdiag-20260529T232542Z-combined-paid-summary.json`. Result: k5 `0/10` and k10 `0/10`; usage was `8,220` tokens; estimated cost about `$0.01436`. This falsifies the "too much top20 context only" hypothesis: the answer needs broader context than k10, but broad context still requires better temporal fact extraction.
- Cutover decision: Stop this candidate-date/cutoff hypothesis. The exact blocker is now narrower: hard LoCoMo temporal questions need structured pre-answer temporal fact extraction or a model-capability strategy that can synthesize the relevant temporal fact from broader context. No Mem0 retirement, LoCoMo30 rerun, or official n30 rerun is justified from these results.

## 2026-05-30 - Kontext optional temporal fact extraction diagnostic

- Summary: Added an explicit optional two-stage temporal path to the judged runner: extract private temporal facts first, then answer from those extracted facts plus retrieved memories. This is off by default and only enabled with `--temporal-fact-extraction`.
- Files touched: `tools/kontext-v2/scripts/judged_benchmark_run.py`, `tools/kontext-v2/tests/test_judged_benchmark_run.py`, and this `project_log.md`.
- Behavior: `ExternalRunConfig` now supports `temporal_fact_extraction` and `omit_temperature`. When enabled for temporal questions, the runner makes one private extraction call per question/cutoff, inserts `Extracted temporal facts:` into the answer prompt, and emits only `temporal_facts_hash` in the public result. Raw extracted facts are not written to public reports. `--omit-temperature` is available for OpenAI models that reject `temperature=0`; default behavior remains temperature `0`.
- Verification: TDD red phase first failed on the missing temporal extraction config/path and then on the missing omit-temperature support. After implementation, `test_judged_benchmark_run.py` passed `26 passed`; focused judged/reporting regression passed `39 passed`; touched Python files compiled; and scoped `git diff --check` passed with only the known `project_log.md` CRLF warning.
- Deploy: Backed up the runner under `/opt/kontext/backups/20260529T233225Z-judged-runner-temporal-extract` for the extraction patch and `/opt/kontext/backups/20260529T233952Z-judged-runner-omit-temperature` for the temperature patch. Deployed the runner to `/opt/kontext/scripts`, `/opt/kontext/src/scripts`, and the running `kontext` container; host/container compiles passed. Container smoke proved the extractor messages and private facts are inserted only in the private prompt path.
- Paid nano extraction diagnostic: Ran GPT-5.4-nano temporal10 extraction mode in two chunks under the existing micro-slice cap. Combined summary: `/opt/kontext/reports/judged-plans/locomo-temporal10-extractv1-20260529T233317Z-combined-paid-summary.json`. k20 reached `6/10` (`0.60`, avg score `0.64`), improving over candidate-date k20 `5/10` but still below the `>=8/10` threshold. Chunk 1 hit an OpenAI `429` during k200 after completing all k20 calls; public partial output remained sanitized.
- Stronger-model diagnostic: One allowed GPT-5 mini extraction-mode chunk was run at k20 only with `--omit-temperature`. Artifact: `/opt/kontext/reports/judged-plans/locomo-temporal10-extractv1-gpt5mini-20260529T234015Z-chunk1-paid-run.json`; verification: `/opt/kontext/reports/judged-plans/locomo-temporal10-extractv1-gpt5mini-20260529T234015Z-chunk1-paid-verification.json`. Result: `4/5` (`0.80`) on the previously weak chunk, provider/usage/model-call gates passed, and no raw payload was emitted. Usage was `111,507` tokens; script estimate was about `$0.0191`, while usage-priced cost at the configured GPT-5 mini rates is about `$0.0477`.
- Cutover decision: This is promising but not enough for n30. Stop paid calls here to respect the small-cap intent. The next justified action is an explicit approval packet for the second GPT-5 mini temporal extraction chunk, then, only if full temporal10 reaches at least `8/10`, a LoCoMo30 approval packet. Mem0 remains backup/rollback; no deletion or official n30 rerun is justified yet.

## 2026-05-30 - Kontext LoCoMo30 GPT-5 mini extraction pass

- Summary: After explicit user approval for a LoCoMo30 GPT-5 mini extraction run capped at `$0.40`, ran the guarded VPS wrapper `/tmp/run_locomo30_extractv1_gpt5mini_safe.sh` with `KONTEXT_APPROVE_LOCOMO30=YES`. The stored OpenAI key was decrypted only inside the local shell process and piped to the remote wrapper; no key value was printed or written to project files.
- Paid result: Artifact `/opt/kontext/reports/judged-plans/locomo30-extractv1-gpt5mini-20260529T235119Z-paid-run.json`; verification `/opt/kontext/reports/judged-plans/locomo30-extractv1-gpt5mini-20260529T235119Z-paid-verification.json`. Aggregate k20 result was `27/30` (`0.90`, avg score `0.911`), provider and model-call verification passed, and total usage was `603,337` tokens. Script-estimated cost remained `$0.11445`, below the approved `$0.40` ceiling.
- Gate status: This closes the LoCoMo30 blocker, but not the whole Mem0 retirement gate. The existing official n30 batch verification still points at the earlier failed LoCoMo artifact and still has missing LongMemEval30 and BEAM30 paid verification reports. No readiness/scorecard/observer result should be treated as green until those remaining suite artifacts are handled or the gate policy is intentionally narrowed in code and documented.
- Observer note: Attempted to rerun `/opt/kontext/scripts/run_kontext_cutover_observer.sh`, but it did not return within three minutes and produced no stdout/stderr before timeout. The spawned remote observer process was killed cleanly. This is an operational blocker to refreshing cutover status and should be diagnosed before any cutover review.
- Observer fix: Root cause isolated to the `docker exec ... kontext_ob1_retrieval_quality_gate.py` substep; usage and client-status subcommands completed in under a second, while the OB1 gate produced no output for 45 seconds. Added `KONTEXT_OB1_RETRIEVAL_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` to bound that substep and generate a sanitized fallback `ob1-retrieval-quality-gate` report with `timeout_or_error` instead of hanging. Local observer tests passed `16 passed`. Deployed only `run_kontext_cutover_observer.sh` to `/opt/kontext/scripts` and `/opt/kontext/src/scripts` with backup `/opt/kontext/backups/20260530T005608Z-observer-runner-timeout`; `bash -n` passed on both remote copies.
- Observer verification: Ran the remote observer with `KONTEXT_OB1_RETRIEVAL_TIMEOUT_SECONDS=20`. It no longer hung; it exited `124` after writing aggregate outputs. Latest observer summary is `pass=6`, `warn=1`, `fail=4`, recommendation `rollback_or_hold_mem0_primary`, remaining work key `production_cutover`. Current attention items include non-green scorecard/readiness, OB1 retrieval quality timeout/failure, official judged gate not proven, and shadow-search quality issues.
- Cutover decision: Kontext remains the primary candidate; Mem0 remains backup/rollback. Next concrete work is to either run guarded LongMemEval30 and BEAM30 official-style paid verifications under an explicit cap, or revise the official judged gate to a documented single-suite LoCoMo30 policy if that is the intended cutover criterion. Do not delete or decommission Mem0.

## 2026-05-30 - Kontext BEAM30 capped retry fails accuracy

- Summary: After explicit user approval for a BEAM30 GPT-5 mini retry capped at `$0.75`, first retried the uncapped-prompt BEAM30 packet three times. - BEAM result: Capped BEAM30 failed accuracy: `17/30` (`0.5667`, avg score `0.5733`) at cutoff 20, below the `0.80` target. Actual usage was `165,486` tokens; script-estimated cost was `$0.10335`, under the approved `$0.75` cap.
- Final batch status: Created `/opt/kontext/reports/judged-plans/judged-n30-gpt5mini-final-batch-approval-20260530T014800Z.json` and `/opt/kontext/reports/judged-plans/judged-n30-gpt5mini-final-batch-verification-20260530T014800Z.json`. Suite results: LoCoMo30 `27/30`, LongMemEval30 `26/30`, BEAM30 `17/30`. This was benchmark-tooling only; no production retrieval, MCP, schema, dashboard, Mem0 runtime/data, DB volumes, or retirement path changed.
- Files touched: `tools/kontext-v2/scripts/judged_benchmark_run.py`, `tools/kontext-v2/scripts/judged_benchmark_approval_packet.py`, `tools/kontext-v2/scripts/beam_failure_audit.py`, `tools/kontext-v2/tests/test_judged_benchmark_run.py`, `tools/kontext-v2/tests/test_judged_benchmark_approval_packet.py`, `tools/kontext-v2/tests/test_beam_failure_audit.py`, and this log.
- Verification: TDD red tests first failed on missing atomizer config, answer prompt wiring, approval-packet cost accounting, and audit classification. After implementation, local checks passed: `test_judged_benchmark_run.py` `88 passed`; packet/audit/batch-verify tests `43 passed`; touched judged scripts compiled; scoped `git diff --check` passed except the known `project_log.md` LF/CRLF warning.
- Deploy: Backed up and deployed touched judged tooling to the VPS under `/opt/kontext/backups/20260601T203534Z-beam-memory-atomizer`. Host/source/container compile checks passed.
- No-call gate: Approval packet `/opt/kontext/reports/judged-plans/beam6-gpt51-rerank-selector-atomizer-offset06-020cap-20260601T203623Z-approval.json` selected `6` questions, estimated `$0.022284`, included the atomizer flag, had no secret in the command template, and public no-call verification reported raw-payload hits `0`.
- Paid BEAM6 result: `/opt/kontext/reports/judged-plans/beam6-gpt51-rerank-selector-atomizer-offset06-020cap-20260601T203623Z-paid-run.json` worsened to `3/6` (`0.5000`, avg score `0.6667`) at cutoff `20`; verification cost, usage, model-call, question-count, provider, and raw-payload gates passed, but accuracy and Mem0-target gates failed. Cost was `$0.019164`.
- Failure audit: `/opt/kontext/reports/judged-plans/beam6-gpt51-rerank-selector-atomizer-offset06-020cap-20260601T203623Z-failure-audit.json` classified all `3` misses as `beam_memory_atomizer_wrong`; coverage remained no-evidence-gap, with `2` structured-loss and `1` synthesis-loss.
- Decision: Reject the memory-atomizer branch and do not run BEAM12/BEAM30. The evidence now rules out local Mem0-style retrieval, selector candidates, state-direct selection, and one-shot memory atomization as sufficient fixes for the hard BEAM slice. Mem0 remains backup/rollback. The next move should be either a formally approved replacement gate for Mem0 retirement or a separate production memory-model redesign, not more BEAM prompt packaging.

## 2026-06-01 - BEAM hard-slice paired Kontext vs legacy Mem0 comparison

- Summary: Built a private legacy-Mem0-offline BEAM6 hard-slice bundle by matching the current hard-slice question hashes from the Kontext bundle, then ran a fair paired judged comparison with the same model, cutoff, prompt flags, judge count, and hard cost cap. This was comparison-only; no production retrieval, MCP, schema, dashboard, Mem0 runtime/data, DB volumes, or retirement path changed.
- Private bundle: `/opt/kontext/private/judged-bundles/beam6-legacy-mem0-offline-hard-offset06-20260601T204426Z-private.json` contains `6` hash-matched questions, backend `legacy-mem0-offline`, top_k `20`.
- Legacy Mem0 no-call gate: `/opt/kontext/reports/judged-plans/beam6-legacy-mem0-offline-selector-hard06-20260601T204505Z-approval.json` selected `6`, estimated `$0.019164`, had no secret in the command template, and public no-call verification reported raw-payload hits `0`.
- Legacy Mem0 paid result: `/opt/kontext/reports/judged-plans/beam6-legacy-mem0-offline-selector-hard06-20260601T204505Z-paid-verification.json` scored `4/6` (`0.6667`, avg score `0.7000`), with raw-payload, usage, model-call, cost, provider, and question-count gates passing but accuracy/Mem0-target gates failing.
- Matching Kontext paid result: `/opt/kontext/reports/judged-plans/beam6-kontext-selector-hard06-paired-20260601T205126Z-paid-verification.json` scored `4/6` (`0.6667`, avg score `0.7167`) on the same six hashes and runner flags, with raw-payload, usage, model-call, cost, provider, and question-count gates passing but accuracy/Mem0-target gates failing.
- Comparison artifact: `/opt/kontext/reports/judged-plans/beam6-kontext-vs-legacy-mem0-hard06-paired-20260601T205126Z-comparison.json` reports delta Kontext minus Mem0 `0.0`, interpretation `inconclusive_within_5pp`, raw-payload hits `0`, and total comparison cost `$0.038328`. The report `ok=false` because both rows failed the BEAM pass threshold, not because of raw leakage.
- Failure audits: Matching audits show both systems failed the same category shape: `1/2` preference following and `1/2` instruction following failed, `0/2` information extraction failed, no evidence-gap class. Kontext misses classified as `2` direct-answer-bypass-not-used; legacy Mem0 misses classified as `2` state-verifier-uncertain. Both show `1` structured-loss and `1` synthesis-loss.
- Decision: This materially weakens BEAM as a Mem0-retirement blocker: the local legacy Mem0 ranker does not pass the same hard slice either. Do not spend on BEAM12/BEAM30 from this evidence. The next defensible path is a replacement quality gate for retirement that combines LoCoMo30, LongMemEval30, paired BEAM evidence showing no Mem0 advantage, MCP canaries, sync proof, backups, and monitored runtime cutover.

## 2026-06-02 - Judged benchmark runner security hardening

- Summary: Implemented the first SMAC security-audit report fixes for judged benchmark tooling without paid calls, deploys, Mem0 changes, or private bundle reads. The batch hardens private debug output path handling, strict verification evidence gates, batch execution stop rules, and LongMemEval failure-audit leak checks.
- Files touched: `tools/kontext-v2/scripts/_private_paths.py`, `judged_benchmark_run.py`, `judged_benchmark_approval_packet.py`, `judged_benchmark_verify.py`, `judged_batch_execute.py`, `judged_batch_verify.py`, `longmemeval_failure_audit.py`, matching focused tests, and this log.
- Changes: Private debug output now uses resolved `/opt/kontext/private` path validation instead of string-prefix checks and writes with exclusive private file creation. Judged verification now requires model-call, usage, provider, answerer-model, judge-model, exact question-count, cost, Mem0-target, accuracy, and raw-payload gates. Batch execution now requires `--max-questions`, validates it against verifier expected questions, applies a wall-clock timeout, tracks actual verified spend after each suite, and marks unrun suites as skipped after a timeout/failure/cost abort. LongMemEval failure audits now scan public reports for raw payload keys and secret-like values before writing, and coverage checks avoid joining all raw memory text into one full string.
- Verification: TDD red tests first failed for traversal paths, missing strict verifier gates, missing batch timeout/cost rails, and unsafe LongMemEval audit writes. Passing checks after implementation: focused benchmark-runner suite `156 passed`; adjacent batch/cutover runbook tests `13 passed`; touched scripts compiled with `py_compile`. No raw private payloads, secrets, paid calls, deploys, DB writes, or Mem0 retirement/deletion actions occurred.
- Decision: The security-runner report batch is complete locally. Next queued SMAC report can now be handled as a separate implementation batch; remote deployment of these runner hardenings should be a separate explicit step with the usual `/opt/kontext` backup, compile, health, and filtered-log checks.

## 2026-06-03 - SMAC 5 deterministic typed-projection fixes

- Summary: Implemented and deployed the deterministic typed-projection fixes from the SMAC report. The projection builder now treats bare cancellations as terminal through their timestamp, resolves newer same-tier value events without collapsing every conflict to ambiguous, collapses extractor replay groups by extractor version before event time, and buckets terminal edges by source event state key. Event hashes now include effective/observed time so legitimate reassertions do not dedupe into older rows. Typed-state rendering now prevents cancelled/ambiguous facts from inheriting stale active metadata values or stale explicit relations. Retrieval routing now merges projection rows for `state_plus_history` queries instead of skipping projection entirely. The benchmark trial now uses full question-hash state keys and scores active/rival events from the same projected value source.
